<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.

</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>

</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes

</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

     2	import copy
     3	import binascii
     4	import hashlib
     5	import hmac
     6	import struct
     7	
     8	from chewie.radius_attributes import ATTRIBUTE_TYPES, Attribute, MessageAuthenticator
     9	from chewie.radius_datatypes import Concat
    10	from chewie.utils import MessageParseError
    11	
    12	RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH = 1 + 1 + 2 + 16
    13	
    14	PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS = {}
    15	
    16	
    17	class InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(Exception):
    18	    """To be used when the ResponseAuthenticator hashes
    19	     (received in packet, and calculated) do not match."""
    20	    pass
    21	
    22	
    23	class InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError(Exception):
    24	    """To be used when the Message-Authenticator hashes
    25	     (received in packet, and calculated) do not match.
    26	    Received packets that throw this error should be 'silently dropped' (logging is fine)."""
    27	    pass

    30	class Radius:
    31	    """Radius packet interface which will determin the correct RadiusPacket child class to use"""
    32	    ACCESS_REQUEST = 1
    33	    ACCESS_ACCEPT = 2
    34	    ACCESS_REJECT = 3
    35	    ACCOUNTING_REQUEST = 4
    36	    ACCOUNTING_RESPONSE = 5
    37	    ACCESS_CHALLENGE = 11
    38	    STATUS_SERVER = 12
    39	    STATUS_CLIENT = 13
    40	
    41	    @staticmethod
    42	    def parse(packed_message, secret, radius_lifecycle=None):
    43	        """
    44	        Args:
    45	            packed_message:
    46	            secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
    47	            radius_lifecycle: RadiusLifecycle object
    48	        Returns:
    49	            RadiusPacket - RadiusAccessChallenge/RadiusAccessRequest/
    50	                            RadiusAccessAccept/RadiusAccessFailure
    51	        Raises:
    52	            MessageParseError: if packed_message cannot be parsed
    53	        """
    54	        try:
    55	            code, packet_id, length, response_authenticator = struct.unpack("!BBH16s",
    56	                                                                            packed_message[:RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH])
    57	        except struct.error as exception:
    58	            raise MessageParseError('Unable to unpack first 20 bytes of RADIUS header') \
    59	                from exception
    60	
    61	        response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(response_authenticator)
    62	        if code in PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS.keys():
    63	            radius_packet = PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS[code](packet_id, response_authenticator,
    64	                                                      RadiusAttributesList.parse(
    65	                                                          packed_message[RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH:]))
    66	            try:
    67	                request_authenticator = radius_lifecycle.packet_id_to_request_authenticator[packet_id]
    68	            except KeyError as exception:
    69	                raise MessageParseError('Unknown RAIDUS packet_id: %s' % packet_id,) \
    70	                    from exception
    71	            try:
    72	                return radius_packet.validate_packet(secret,
    73	                                                     request_authenticator=request_authenticator)
    74	            except (InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError,
    75	                    InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError) as exception:
    76	                raise MessageParseError("Unable to parse Radius packet") \
    77	                    from exception
    78	        raise MessageParseError("Unable to parse radius code: %d" % code)
    79	
    80	    def pack(self):
    81	        pass
    82	
    83	
    84	def register_packet_type_parser(cls):
    85	    PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS[cls.CODE] = cls.parse
    86	    return cls
    87	
    88	
    89	class RadiusPacket(Radius):
    90	    """super class for different radius packets"""
    91	    CODE = None
    92	    packed = None
    93	
    94	    def __init__(self, packet_id, authenticator, attributes):
    95	        self.packet_id = packet_id
    96	        self.authenticator = authenticator
    97	        self.attributes = attributes
    98	
    99	    @classmethod
   100	    def parse(cls, packet_id, request_authenticator, attributes):
   101	        return cls(packet_id, request_authenticator, attributes)
   102	
   103	    def pack(self):
   104	        header = struct.pack("!BBH16s", self.CODE, self.packet_id,
   105	                             RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH + self.attributes.__len__(),
   106	                             self.authenticator)
   107	        packed_attributes = self.attributes.pack()
   108	        self.packed = bytearray(header + packed_attributes)
   109	        return self.packed
   110	
   111	    def build(self, secret=None):
   112	        """Only call this once, or else the MessageAuthenticator will not be zeros,
   113	         resulting in the wrong hash
   114	         Args:
   115	             secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
   116	        Returns:
   117	            packed packet (bytes)"""
   118	        if not self.packed:
   119	            self.pack()
   120	        try:
   121	            position = self.attributes.indexof(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION) + \
   122	                       RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH + Attribute.HEADER_SIZE
   123	        except ValueError as e:
   124	            print(e.message)
   125	            return self.packed
   126	
   127	        if secret:
   128	            message_authenticator = bytearray(hmac.new(secret.encode(), self.packed, 'md5')
   129	                                              .digest())
   130	
   131	            for i in range(16):
   132	                self.packed[i+position] = message_authenticator[i]
   133	        return self.packed
   134	
   135	    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):
   136	        """Calculates the Response Authenticator (in Radius Header) and
   137	        MessageAuthenticator (a Radius Attribute) hashes and compares with what was provided.
   138	        Args:
   139	            secret (str): secret shared between RADIUS and chewie.
   140	            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
   141	             packet (which is a response)
   142	        Raises:
   143	            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
   144	            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
   145	            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
   146	        """
   147	        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
   148	        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
   149	        # get the Original Message Authenticator
   150	
   151	        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)
   152	        if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:
   153	            # no validation required.
   154	            return self
   155	        if not secret:
   156	            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")
   157	
   158	        response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
   159	        radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
   160	        radius_packet.pack()
   161	        calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
   162	                                                             bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
   163	        if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:
   164	            raise InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(
   165	                "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
   166	                    response_authenticator,
   167	                    calculated_response_authenticator,
   168	                    binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))
   169	
   170	        original_ma = message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data
   171	        # Replace the Original Message Authenticator
   172	        message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data = bytes.fromhex("00000000000000000000000000000000")
   173	        radius_packet.pack()
   174	
   175	        # calculate new hash message authenticator
   176	        new_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), radius_packet.packed, 'md5').digest()
   177	
   178	        # compare old and new message authenticator
   179	        if original_ma != new_ma:
   180	            raise InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError(
   181	                "Original Message-Authenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s'" %
   182	                (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))
   183	        return self
   184	
   185	
   186	@register_packet_type_parser
   187	class RadiusAccessRequest(RadiusPacket):
   188	    CODE = Radius.ACCESS_REQUEST
   189	
   190	
   191	@register_packet_type_parser
   192	class RadiusAccessAccept(RadiusPacket):
   193	    CODE = Radius.ACCESS_ACCEPT
   194	
   195	
   196	@register_packet_type_parser
   197	class RadiusAccessReject(RadiusPacket):
   198	    CODE = Radius.ACCESS_REJECT
   199

   201	@register_packet_type_parser
   202	class RadiusAccessChallenge(RadiusPacket):
   203	    CODE = Radius.ACCESS_CHALLENGE
   204	
   205	
   206	class RadiusAttributesList:
   207	    """Container class for the Radius Attribute Value Pairs"""
   208	
   209	    def __init__(self, attributes):
   210	        self.attributes = attributes
   211	
   212	    @classmethod
   213	    def parse(cls, attributes_data):
   214	        """
   215	
   216	        Args:
   217	            attributes_data:
   218	
   219	        Returns:
   220	            RadiusAttributeList
   221	        Raises:
   222	            MessageParseError: if unable to parse an attribute's data.
   223	        """
   224	        attributes = []
   225	        attributes_to_concat = {}
   226	        cls.extract_attributes(attributes_data, attributes, attributes_to_concat)
   227	
   228	        attributes = cls.merge_concat_attributes(attributes, attributes_to_concat)
   229	
   230	        return cls(attributes)
   231	
   232	    @classmethod
   233	    def merge_concat_attributes(cls, attributes, attributes_to_concat):
   234	        """
   235	        Removes concat attributes for attributes list, and inserts a single new master attribute
   236	        for all concat attributes of the same type (e.g. EAPMessage, EAPMessage, = 1 EAPMessage)
   237	        Args:
   238	            attributes (list):
   239	            attributes_to_concat (dict): attribute - position.
   240	        Returns:
   241	            attributes (list)
   242	        Raises:
   243	            MessageParseError: RadiusAttribute.parse will raise error
   244	            if it cannot parse the attribute's data
   245	        """
   246	        # Join Attributes that's datatype is Concat into one attribute.
   247	        concatenated_attributes = []
   248	        for value, list_ in attributes_to_concat.items():
   249	            concatenated_data = b""
   250	            for d, i in list_:
   251	                concatenated_data += d.data_type.bytes_data
   252	            concatenated_attributes.append(tuple((ATTRIBUTE_TYPES[value].parse(concatenated_data),
   253	                                                  i)))
   254	        # Remove old Attributes that were concatenated.
   255	        for ca, _ in concatenated_attributes:
   256	            attributes = [x for x in attributes if x.TYPE != ca.TYPE]
   257	
   258	        # need to put them back in the same position.
   259	        for ca, i in concatenated_attributes:
   260	            attributes.insert(i, ca)
   261	
   262	        return attributes
   263	
   264	    @classmethod
   265	    def extract_attributes(cls, attributes_data, attributes, attributes_to_concat):
   266	        """
   267	        Extracts Radius Attributes from a packed payload.
   268	        Keeps track of attribute ordering.
   269	        Args:
   270	            attributes_data (): data to extract from (input).
   271	            attributes: attributes extracted (output variable).
   272	            attributes_to_concat (dict): (output variable).
   273	        Raises:
   274	            MessageParseError: RadiusAttribute.parse will raise error
   275	            if it cannot parse the attribute's data
   276	        """
   277	        total_length = len(attributes_data)
   278	        pos = 0
   279	        index = -1
   280	        last_attribute = -1
   281	        while pos < total_length:
   282	            try:
   283	                type_, attr_length = struct.unpack("!BB",
   284	                                                   attributes_data[pos:pos + Attribute.HEADER_SIZE])
   285	            except struct.error as exception:
   286	                raise MessageParseError('Unable to unpack first 2 bytes of attribute header')\
   287	                    from exception
   288	            data = attributes_data[pos + Attribute.HEADER_SIZE: pos + attr_length]
   289	            pos += attr_length
   290	
   291	            packed_value = data[:attr_length - Attribute.HEADER_SIZE]
   292	            try:
   293	                attribute = ATTRIBUTE_TYPES[type_].parse(packed_value)
   294	            except KeyError as exception:
   295	                raise MessageParseError('Cannot find parser for RADIUS attribute %s' %
   296	                                        type_) from exception
   297	            # keep track of where the concated AVP should be in the attributes list.
   298	            # required so the hashing gives correct hash.
   299	            if attribute.DATA_TYPE != Concat or last_attribute != attribute.TYPE:
   300	                index += 1
   301	            last_attribute = attribute.TYPE
   302	
   303	            if attribute.DATA_TYPE.DATA_TYPE_VALUE == Concat.DATA_TYPE_VALUE:
   304	                if attribute.TYPE not in attributes_to_concat:
   305	                    attributes_to_concat[attribute.TYPE] = []
   306	                attributes_to_concat[attribute.TYPE].append((attribute, index))
   307	
   308	            attributes.append(attribute)
   309	
   310	    def find(self, item):
   311	        """Find first attribute that has the matching description
   312	        Args:
   313	            item (str): description of attribute to find
   314	        Returns:
   315	            attribute or None if not found"""
   316	        for attr in self.attributes:
   317	            if item == attr.DESCRIPTION:
   318	                return attr
   319	        return None
   320	
   321	    def indexof(self, item):
   322	        """Finds the position (number of bytes) that item is at in list.
   323	        Args:
   324	            item (str): description of attribute to find index of.
   325	        Returns:
   326	            int - number of bytes to item.
   327	        Raises:
   328	            ValueErrpr: if cannot find item
   329	        """
   330	        i = 0
   331	        for attr in self.attributes:
   332	            if item == attr.DESCRIPTION:
   333	                break
   334	            i += attr.full_length()
   335	        else:
   336	            raise ValueError("Cannot find item: %s in attributes list" % item)
   337	        return i
   338	
   339	    def __len__(self):
   340	        total = 0
   341	        for attr in self.attributes:
   342	            total = total + attr.full_length()
   343	        return total
   344	
   345	    def pack(self):
   346	        packed_attributes = bytes()
   347	        for attr in self.attributes:
   348	            packed_attributes += attr.pack()
   349	        return packed_attributes
   350	
   351	    def to_dict(self):
   352	        ret = {}
   353	        for a in self.attributes:
   354	            ret[a.DESCRIPTION] = a.data_type.data()
   355	        return ret


This means that if EITHER message_authenticator is missing OR request_authenticator is missing, no validation is performed at all.

2. According to the issue description, the correct logic should be:
```python

    validate message_authenticator
if request_authenticator:
    validate request_authenticator
```

This means:
- If message_authenticator exists, validate it
- If request_authenticator exists, validate it
- These validations should be independent - one missing shouldn't prevent validating the other

**Problem Restatement:** 
The current RADIUS packet validation logic incorrectly skips all validation when either the Message-Authenticator or request_authenticator is missing. According to the issue, the validation should be independent - if Message-Authenticator exists, validate it; if request_authenticator exists, validate it. Missing one should not prevent validating the other.

**Key Technical Details:**
- **File:** `/workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0/chewie/radius.py`
- **Method:** `validate_packet` in `RadiusPacket` class  
- **Lines:** 151-154
- **Variables:** `message_authenticator`, `request_authenticator`
- **Current problematic logic:** `if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:`

**Clear Problem Statement:**
The validation logic uses an "or" condition that prevents any validation when either authenticator is missing, but it should allow independent validation of each authenticator when present.

**Reproduction Steps:**
1. Create a RADIUS packet with a valid request_authenticator but no Message-Authenticator
2. Call validate_packet() 
3. Current behavior: No validation occurs
4. Expected behavior: request_authenticator should still be validated

**Best Practices for Fix:**
- Make minimal, focused changes
- Separate validation logic for independent authenticators
- Preserve existing error handling and validation flow
- Ensure backward compatibility

     1	# Chewie
     2	
     3	[![Build Status](https://travis-ci.com/faucetsdn/chewie.svg?branch=master)](https://travis-ci.com/faucetsdn/chewie)
     4	[![Maintainability](https://api.codeclimate.com/v1/badges/66b6e93ba93b6ac56d17/maintainability)](https://codeclimate.com/github/faucetsdn/chewie/maintainability)
     5	[![Test Coverage](https://api.codeclimate.com/v1/badges/66b6e93ba93b6ac56d17/test_coverage)](https://codeclimate.com/github/faucetsdn/chewie/test_coverage)
     6	
     7	## Chewie - EAPOL / 802.1x
     8	Chewie is an EAPOL/802.1x implementation in Python.
     9	It is designed to work on it's own but primarily as a module for [The Faucet Project](https://github.com/faucetsdn/faucet)
    10	which is an open-source SDN controller implementation in Python.
    11	
    12	### Supported Features:
    13	* PEAP
    14	* MD5-SUM   
    15	* TLS
    16	* TTLS
    17	
    18	 
    19	## Configuration
    20	Setting up credentials with Chewie can be set on the Radius server, if using the default configuration this can be found
    21	in the `etc/freeradius/users` file.
    22	
    23	The default credentials for the username and password are `user` and `microphone` respectively.
    24	Example authentication certificates for TLS / TTLS / PEAP have been provided in the `etc` folder. 
    25	
    26	NOTE: These are self-signed certificates
    27	
    28	
    29	## Getting Started:
    30	
    31	Getting started with Chewie is as easy as starting a docker-compose network. This has been described below.
    32	If you would like to learn about the requirements for running Chewie, all of the dependencies for Chewie have been 
    33	defined in the `Dockerfile.chewie` file, with the `pip` dependencies defined in the `requirements.txt` and 
    34	`test-requirements.txt` files respectively.
    35	
    36	### Docker / Docker-Compose:
    37	
    38	#### Setup
    39	
    40	If needed, installation instructions for [Docker](https://store.docker.com/) and [Docker-Compose](https://docs.docker.com/compose/) can be
    41	found on the official Docker website or by following the links provided.
    42	
    43	#### Starting a Docker-Compose
    44	
    45	##### To run Chewie in the Docker-Compose Environment:
    46	
    47	`docker-compose up --build`
    48	
    49	##### To Stop and Clean Up the Docker Environment
    50	
    51	`docker-compose down`
    52	
    53	## Development 
    54	
    55	## Building the Docker Image
    56	
    57	For a developer to build and run the Chewie inside a Docker it is easy to pass through the live source-code to the Docker 
    58	container, allowing for the developer to restart Chewie and code changes to be active on application restart.
    59	
    60	To get started working with `Chewie`, the main image needs to be built before a developer can run it.
    61	
    62	`docker build -t chewie_image -f docker/Dockerfile.chewie .`
    63	
    64	To start the docker image inside a container, mounting the `Chewie` source code to `/chewie/` the instruction below is provided:
    65	
    66	`docker run -it -v $(pwd)/:/chewie/:ro chewie_image /bin/bash`
    67	
    68	To run Chewie once in the Docker environment:
    69	`python3 /chewie/run.py`
    70	
    71	#### Testing
    72	
    73	##### Running unit-tests in Docker 
    74	
    75	To build and run the Chewie test-suite inside a Docker:
    76	
    77	```
    78	docker build -t chewie_test_image -f docker/Dockerfile.test .
    79	docker run -t chewie_test_image
    80	```
    81	
    82	
    83	#### Questions / Bugs
    84	
    85	If there are any questions or bugs found please report them to the Chewie project via the issue link.
    86	This can be found at 
    87	[https://github.com/faucetsdn/chewie/issues](https://github.com/faucetsdn/chewie/issues)
    88

    86	    def test_corrupted_packets(self):
    87	
    88	        # the original response authenticator does not match the computed one
    89	        #  because there is a change in the packet contents
    90	        packed_message = bytes.fromhex(

    97	                          }))
    98	            self.fail()

   135	    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):
   136	        """Calculates the Response Authenticator (in Radius Header) and
   137	        MessageAuthenticator (a Radius Attribute) hashes and compares with what was provided.
   138	        Args:
   139	            secret (str): secret shared between RADIUS and chewie.
   140	            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
   141	             packet (which is a response)
   142	        Raises:
   143	            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
   144	            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
   145	            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
   146	        """
   147	        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
   148	        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
   149	        # get the Original Message Authenticator
   150	
   151	        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)
   152	        if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:
   153	            # no validation required.
   154	            return self
   155	        if not secret:
   156	            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")
   157	
   158	        response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
   159	        radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
   160	        radius_packet.pack()
   161	        calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
   162	                                                             bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
   163	        if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:
   164	            raise InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(
   165	                "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
   166	                    response_authenticator,
   167	                    calculated_response_authenticator,
   168	                    binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))
   169	
   170	        original_ma = message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data
   171	        # Replace the Original Message Authenticator
   172	        message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data = bytes.fromhex("00000000000000000000000000000000")
   173	        radius_packet.pack()
   174	
   175	        # calculate new hash message authenticator
   176	        new_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), radius_packet.packed, 'md5').digest()
   177	
   178	        # compare old and new message authenticator
   179	        if original_ma != new_ma:
   180	            raise InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError(
   181	                "Original Message-Authenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s'" %
   182	                (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))
   183	        return self
   184

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### Summary of Exploration

**Files related to the problem:**
1. **Primary file to fix:** `/workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0/chewie/radius.py` - Contains the `validate_packet` method with the faulty logic
2. **Test files:** `/workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0/test/test_radius.py` - Contains existing tests for RADIUS functionality

**Methods to fix:**
- `validate_packet` method in `RadiusPacket` class (lines 135-183)

**Root cause:**
Line 152: `if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:` uses OR logic, preventing validation when either authenticator is missing.

**Solution approach:**
Separate the validation logic so that:
1. If message_authenticator exists, validate it
2. If request_authenticator exists, validate it
3. Both validations can occur independently

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Reproducing the Issue<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the existing tests, I need to understand what kind of scenario I should create to reproduce the issue:

1. The problem occurs when we have a valid request_authenticator but no message_authenticator
2. In the current implementation, validation is skipped entirely
3. With the fix, the request_authenticator should still be validated

Let me look at the existing test structure to understand how to create a proper test case.

From the test file, I can see:
- Tests use Radius.parse() method which calls validate_packet internally
- Tests use namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator') to mock the lifecycle
- Tests pass packed messages and secrets
- Tests expect either successful parsing or specific exceptions

To reproduce the issue, I need to:
1. Create a RADIUS packet that has a valid request_authenticator (so response authenticator validation should work)
2. But doesn't have a Message-Authenticator attribute
3. With the current code, no validation happens at all
4. With the fixed code, the request_authenticator should still be validated

Actually, wait - let me re-read the issue more carefully. The issue says that if Message-Authenticator doesn't exist, we should still check the request_authenticator. 

So the current behavior is:
- If either message_authenticator OR request_authenticator is missing -> skip ALL validation
- Desired behavior:
  - If message_authenticator exists -> validate it
  - If request_authenticator exists -> validate it
  - They should be validated independently

So I need to create a test case where:
1. message_authenticator is None/missing
2. request_authenticator is valid and present
3. The response authenticator should still be validated

Or vice versa:
1. message_authenticator is valid and present  
2. request_authenticator is None/missing
3. The message authenticator should still be validated

But wait, looking at the code more carefully, I see that the response authenticator validation depends on having a request_authenticator to reconstruct the packet. So if request_authenticator is missing, we can't validate the response authenticator.

Let me trace through the logic:
1. Line 158: response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator (this is the received response authenticator)
2. Line 159: radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator (we replace it with the original request authenticator)
3. Line 160: radius_packet.pack() (repack with the original request authenticator)
4. Line 161-162: Calculate what the response authenticator SHOULD be
5. Line 163-168: Compare actual vs calculated response authenticator

So indeed, we need request_authenticator to validate the response authenticator.

But the message authenticator validation is independent - it validates the HMAC of the entire packet.

So the scenarios are:
1. message_authenticator missing, request_authenticator present -> should validate response authenticator
2. message_authenticator present, request_authenticator missing -> should validate message authenticator only
3. Both present -> should validate both
4. Both missing -> no validation needed

The current code fails scenario 1.

"""
Test script to reproduce the Message-Authenticator validation issue.

Issue: If Message-Authenticator doesn't exist we should still check the request_authenticator
"""

import binascii
from collections import namedtuple
from chewie.radius import Radius, RadiusAccessAccept, RadiusAttributesList, InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError
from chewie.radius_attributes import UserName, EAPMessage, MessageAuthenticator


def test_current_behavior_with_missing_message_authenticator():
    """Test that demonstrates the current problematic behavior."""
    print("=== Testing Current Behavior ===")
    
    # Create a valid RADIUS Access Accept packet WITHOUT Message-Authenticator
    # This mimics a real-world scenario where Message-Authenticator is optional
    
    # Packet structure:
    # Code (1 byte): 02 (Access-Accept)
    # Identifier (1 byte): 01 
    # Length (2 bytes): 0046 (70 bytes)
    # Authenticator (16 bytes): 02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2410
    # Attributes:
    #   - Vendor-Specific: 1a0f00003039010973747564656e74
    #   - EAP-Message: 4f0603020004  
    #   - User-Name: 010b686f73743175736572
    
    # This is similar to test_radius_access_accept_parses but WITHOUT Message-Authenticator
    packed_message = bytes.fromhex("0201004602970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d24101a0f00003039010973747564656e744f0603020004010b686f73743175736572")
    
    # Valid request authenticator (needed for response authenticator validation)
    request_authenticator = bytes.fromhex("a0b4ace0b367114b1a16d76e2bfed5d8")
    
    print(f"Packed message: {packed_message.hex()}")
    print(f"Request authenticator: {request_authenticator.hex()}")
    
    try:
        # This should validate the response authenticator since we have a valid request_authenticator
        # But currently it skips ALL validation because Message-Authenticator is missing
        message = Radius.parse(packed_message, secret="SECRET",
                               radius_lifecycle=namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator')({
                                   1: request_authenticator
                               }))
        print("✓ Parse succeeded - but this might be incorrect behavior!")
        print(f"  Packet ID: {message.packet_id}")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Parse failed with: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        return False


def test_should_fail_with_invalid_response_authenticator():
    """Test that should fail when response authenticator is invalid."""
    print("\n=== Testing Invalid Response Authenticator ===")
    
    # Same packet as above but with corrupted response authenticator
    # Original authenticator: 02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2410
    # Corrupted authenticator: 02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2411 (last byte changed)
    packed_message = bytes.fromhex("0201004602970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d24111a0f00003039010973747564656e744f0603020004010b686f73743175736572")
    
    request_authenticator = bytes.fromhex("a0b4ace0b367114b1a16d76e2bfed5d8")
    
    print(f"Packed message (corrupted): {packed_message.hex()}")
    print(f"Request authenticator: {request_authenticator.hex()}")
    
    try:
        message = Radius.parse(packed_message, secret="SECRET",
                               radius_lifecycle=namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator')({
                                   1: request_authenticator
                               }))
        print("✗ Parse succeeded - this indicates the bug! Response authenticator validation was skipped.")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        if "InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError" in str(type(e)):
            print("✓ Parse correctly failed with InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError")
            print(f"  Error: {e}")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Parse failed with unexpected error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            return False


def test_create_packet_without_message_authenticator():
    """Create a packet programmatically without Message-Authenticator."""
    print("\n=== Creating Packet Without Message-Authenticator ===")
    
    # Create attributes without Message-Authenticator
    attr_list = []
    attr_list.append(EAPMessage.create("03020004"))  # Success message
    attr_list.append(UserName.create("host1user"))
    
    attributes = RadiusAttributesList(attr_list)
    access_accept = RadiusAccessAccept(1, bytes.fromhex("02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2410"), attributes)
    
    # Build the packet
    packed_message = access_accept.pack()
    print(f"Created packed message: {packed_message.hex()}")
    
    # Check if Message-Authenticator is actually missing
    found_ma = attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)
    print(f"Message-Authenticator found: {found_ma is not None}")
    
    return packed_message


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Reproducing Message-Authenticator validation issue...")
    
    # Test 1: Current behavior with missing Message-Authenticator
    success1 = test_current_behavior_with_missing_message_authenticator()
    
    # Test 2: Should fail with invalid response authenticator but might not due to bug
    success2 = test_should_fail_with_invalid_response_authenticator()
    
    # Test 3: Create packet without Message-Authenticator
    packed_msg = test_create_packet_without_message_authenticator()

    if not success2:
        print("\n🐛 BUG CONFIRMED: Response authenticator validation is being skipped!")
        print("   This happens because Message-Authenticator is missing.")
    else:
        print("\n✅ Response authenticator validation is working correctly.")</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0/test_reproduce_issue.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0 && python test_reproduce_issue.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Traceback (most recent call last):

    #   - Vendor-Specific: 1a0f00003039010973747564656e74
    #   - EAP-Message: 4f0603020004  
    #   - User-Name: 010b686f73743175736572
    
    # This is similar to test_radius_access_accept_parses but WITHOUT Message-Authenticator
    packed_message = bytes.fromhex("0201004602970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d24101a0f00003039010973747564656e744f0603020004010b686f73743175736572")
    
    # Valid request authenticator (needed for response authenticator validation)
    request_authenticator = bytes.fromhex("a0b4ace0b367114b1a16d76e2bfed5d8")
    
    print(f"Packed message: {packed_message.hex()}")
    print(f"Request authenticator: {request_authenticator.hex()}")
    
    try:
        # This should validate the response authenticator since we have a valid request_authenticator
        # But currently it skips ALL validation because Message-Authenticator is missing
        message = Radius.parse(packed_message, secret="SECRET",
                               radius_lifecycle=namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator')({
                                   1: request_authenticator
                               }))
        print("✓ Parse succeeded - but this might be incorrect behavior!")
        print(f"  Packet ID: {message.packet_id}")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Parse failed with: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        return False


def test_should_fail_with_invalid_response_authenticator():
    """Test that should fail when response authenticator is invalid."""
    print("\n=== Testing Invalid Response Authenticator ===")
    
    # Import after the package is properly loaded
    from chewie.radius import Radius
    from chewie.radius_attributes import MessageAuthenticator
    
    # Same packet as above but with corrupted response authenticator
    # Original authenticator: 02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2410
    # Corrupted authenticator: 02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2411 (last byte changed)
    packed_message = bytes.fromhex("0201004602970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d24111a0f00003039010973747564656e744f0603020004010b686f73743175736572")
    
    request_authenticator = bytes.fromhex("a0b4ace0b367114b1a16d76e2bfed5d8")
    
    print(f"Packed message (corrupted): {packed_message.hex()}")
    print(f"Request authenticator: {request_authenticator.hex()}")
    
    try:
        message = Radius.parse(packed_message, secret="SECRET",
                               radius_lifecycle=namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator')({
                                   1: request_authenticator
                               }))
        print("✗ Parse succeeded - this indicates the bug! Response authenticator validation was skipped.")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        if "InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError" in str(type(e)):
            print("✓ Parse correctly failed with InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError")
            print(f"  Error: {e}")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Parse failed with unexpected error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            return False


def test_create_packet_without_message_authenticator():
    """Create a packet programmatically without Message-Authenticator."""
    print("\n=== Creating Packet Without Message-Authenticator ===")
    
    # Import after the package is properly loaded
    from chewie.radius import RadiusAccessAccept, RadiusAttributesList
    from chewie.radius_attributes import UserName, EAPMessage, MessageAuthenticator
    
    # Create attributes without Message-Authenticator
    attr_list = []
    attr_list.append(EAPMessage.create("03020004"))  # Success message
    attr_list.append(UserName.create("host1user"))
    
    attributes = RadiusAttributesList(attr_list)
    access_accept = RadiusAccessAccept(1, bytes.fromhex("02970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d2410"), attributes)
    
    # Build the packet
    packed_message = access_accept.pack()
    print(f"Created packed message: {packed_message.hex()}")
    
    # Check if Message-Authenticator is actually missing
    found_ma = attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)
    print(f"Message-Authenticator found: {found_ma is not None}")
    
    return packed_message

def test_missing_message_authenticator():
    """Test that shows the bug: when Message-Authenticator is missing, 
    request_authenticator validation is also skipped."""
    
    print("Testing RADIUS packet validation with missing Message-Authenticator...")
    
    # Create a RADIUS Access Accept packet WITHOUT Message-Authenticator
    # This is a legitimate scenario - Message-Authenticator is optional in RADIUS
    packed_message = bytes.fromhex("0201004602970aff2ef0700780f70848e90d24101a0f00003039010973747564656e744f0603020004010b686f73743175736572")
    
    # Valid request authenticator - this SHOULD be validated
    request_authenticator = bytes.fromhex("a0b4ace0b367114b1a16d76e2bfed5d8")
    
    print(f"Packet has Message-Authenticator: {'5012' in packed_message.hex()}")
    print(f"Request authenticator provided: {request_authenticator is not None}")
    
    try:
        # Parse the packet - this internally calls validate_packet()
        message = Radius.parse(packed_message, secret="SECRET",
                               radius_lifecycle=namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator')({
                                   1: request_authenticator
                               }))
        print("✓ Parse succeeded")
        print("  This might indicate the bug - validation may have been skipped!")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Parse failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        return False

    # Valid request authenticator - validation SHOULD catch the invalid response authenticator
    request_authenticator = bytes.fromhex("a0b4ace0b367114b1a16d76e2bfed5d8")
    
    try:
        message = Radius.parse(packed_message, secret="SECRET",
                               radius_lifecycle=namedtuple('RadiusLifecycle', 'packet_id_to_request_authenticator')({
                                   1: request_authenticator
                               }))
        print("✗ Parse succeeded - BUG CONFIRMED!")

        else:

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly state what I understand about the problem:

**5.1 What the problem is:**
The current validation logic in `validate_packet` method (line 152) uses:
```python
if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:
    # no validation required.
    return self
```

This means that if EITHER the Message-Authenticator is missing OR the request_authenticator is missing, ALL validation is skipped. This is incorrect behavior.

**5.2 Where the problem is located:**
File: `/workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0/chewie/radius.py`
Method: `validate_packet` in `RadiusPacket` class
Line: 152

**5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:**
While I couldn't run my custom tests due to circular import issues, the concept is:
1. Create a RADIUS packet with a valid request_authenticator but no Message-Authenticator attribute
2. Parse the packet with a valid secret and request_authenticator
3. Currently: No validation occurs at all (bug)
4. Expected: The response authenticator should still be validated using the provided request_authenticator

**5.4 Best practices for the fix:**
- Make minimal changes to fix the core issue
- Separate the validation logic for independent authenticators
- Preserve existing error handling and validation flow
- Don't break backward compatibility
- Ensure both validations can occur independently

**5.5 How to fix the problem:**
Instead of:
```python
if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:
    return self
```

We should have:
```python
# Validate response authenticator if we have request_authenticator
if request_authenticator:
    # ... validate response authenticator ...
    
# Validate message authenticator if we have message_authenticator  
if message_authenticator:
    # ... validate message authenticator ...

# If neither authenticator is present, no validation needed
if not request_authenticator and not message_authenticator:
    return self
```

Actually, looking more carefully at the code, the response authenticator validation depends on having the request_authenticator, and the message authenticator validation depends on having the message_authenticator. So the fix should be:

1. If request_authenticator exists, validate the response authenticator
2. If message_authenticator exists, validate the message authenticator
3. Both validations should happen independently
4. Only require the secret if we're doing actual validation

The key insight is that we should not skip validation just because one of the authenticators is missing - we should validate whichever ones we can.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The current validation logic in the `validate_packet` method incorrectly skips ALL validation when either the Message-Authenticator OR the request_authenticator is missing. According to the issue description, validation should be independent - if Message-Authenticator exists, validate it; if request_authenticator exists, validate it.

### 5.2 Problem Location
- **File:** `/workspace/faucetsdn__chewie__0.0/chewie/radius.py`
- **Method:** `validate_packet` in `RadiusPacket` class  
- **Line:** 152
- **Code:** `if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:`

### 5.3 Test Reproduction Concept
While circular imports prevented running custom tests, the issue manifests when:
1. A RADIUS packet has a valid request_authenticator but no Message-Authenticator attribute
2. Parsing should validate the response authenticator using the provided request_authenticator
3. Currently: All validation is skipped due to missing Message-Authenticator (bug)
4. Expected: Response authenticator validation should still occur

### 5.4 Best Practices for Fix
- Make minimal, focused changes to address the core logic issue
- Separate validation logic for independent authenticators
- Preserve existing error handling and validation flow
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Enable independent validation of each authenticator

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Replace the single "or" condition with independent validation checks:
1. If `request_authenticator` exists → validate response authenticator
2. If `message_authenticator` exists → validate message authenticator  
3. Both validations occur independently based on what's available
4. Only require secret when performing actual validations

        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
        # get the Original Message Authenticator

        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)

        response_validation_needed = request_authenticator is not None
        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided

                    (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))

    @staticmethod
    def parse(packed_message, secret, radius_lifecycle=None):
        """
        Args:
            packed_message:
            secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
            radius_lifecycle: RadiusLifecycle object
        Returns:
            RadiusPacket - RadiusAccessChallenge/RadiusAccessRequest/
                            RadiusAccessAccept/RadiusAccessFailure
        Raises:
            MessageParseError: if packed_message cannot be parsed
        """
        try:
            code, packet_id, length, response_authenticator = struct.unpack("!BBH16s",
                                                                            packed_message[:RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH])
        except struct.error as exception:
            raise MessageParseError('Unable to unpack first 20 bytes of RADIUS header') \
                from exception

        response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(response_authenticator)
        if code in PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS.keys():
            radius_packet = PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS[code](packet_id, response_authenticator,
                                                      RadiusAttributesList.parse(
                                                          packed_message[RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH:]))
            try:
                request_authenticator = radius_lifecycle.packet_id_to_request_authenticator[packet_id]
            except KeyError as exception:
                raise MessageParseError('Unknown RAIDUS packet_id: %s' % packet_id,) \
                    from exception
            try:
                return radius_packet.validate_packet(secret,

    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):
        """Calculates the Response Authenticator (in Radius Header) and
        MessageAuthenticator (a Radius Attribute) hashes and compares with what was provided.
        Args:
            secret (str): secret shared between RADIUS and chewie.
            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
             packet (which is a response)
        Raises:
            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
        """
        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
        # get the Original Message Authenticator

        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        response_validation_needed = request_authenticator is not None
        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        message_validation_needed = message_authenticator is not None

        # If no validation is needed, return early
        if not response_validation_needed and not message_validation_needed:
            return self

        if not secret:
            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        if response_validation_needed:
            response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
            radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
            radius_packet.pack()
            calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
                                                                 bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
            if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:
                raise InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
                        response_authenticator,
                        calculated_response_authenticator,
                        binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))

        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        if message_validation_needed:
            original_ma = message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data
            # Replace the Original Message Authenticator
            message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data = bytes.fromhex("00000000000000000000000000000000")
            radius_packet.pack()

            # calculate new hash message authenticator
            new_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), radius_packet.packed, 'md5').digest()

>                   (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))

    @staticmethod
    def parse(packed_message, secret, radius_lifecycle=None):
        """
        Args:
            packed_message:
            secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
            radius_lifecycle: RadiusLifecycle object
        Returns:
            RadiusPacket - RadiusAccessChallenge/RadiusAccessRequest/
                            RadiusAccessAccept/RadiusAccessFailure
        Raises:
            MessageParseError: if packed_message cannot be parsed
        """
        try:
            code, packet_id, length, response_authenticator = struct.unpack("!BBH16s",
                                                                            packed_message[:RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH])
        except struct.error as exception:
            raise MessageParseError('Unable to unpack first 20 bytes of RADIUS header') \
                from exception

        response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(response_authenticator)
        if code in PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS.keys():
            radius_packet = PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS[code](packet_id, response_authenticator,
                                                      RadiusAttributesList.parse(
                                                          packed_message[RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH:]))
            try:
                request_authenticator = radius_lifecycle.packet_id_to_request_authenticator[packet_id]
            except KeyError as exception:
                raise MessageParseError('Unknown RAIDUS packet_id: %s' % packet_id,) \
                    from exception
            try:
                return radius_packet.validate_packet(secret,
                                                     request_authenticator=request_authenticator)
            except (InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError,
                    InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError) as exception:
                raise MessageParseError("Unable to parse Radius packet") \
>                   from exception

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>I see! The build method calculates the Message-Authenticator by:
1. Packing the packet with the Message-Authenticator field as zeros (or whatever it was initialized with)
2. Calculating HMAC-MD5 of the entire packed packet
3. Putting that result into the Message-Authenticator field

But in validation, we need to:
1. Take the received packet 
2. Zero out the Message-Authenticator field
3. Calculate HMAC-MD5 of that modified packet
4. Compare with the original Message-Authenticator value

    packed_original = bytes.fromhex("010000a3982a0ba06d3557f0dbc8ba6e823822f1010b686f737431757365721e1434342d34342d34342d34342d34342d34343a3d06000000130606000000021f1330302d30302d30302d31312d31312d30314d17434f4e4e45435420304d627073203830322e3131622c12433634383030344139433930353537390c06000005784f100201000e01686f73743175736572501...573")

    print("Message-Authenticator starts at position:", ma_start)

        packed_for_calc = bytearray(packed_original)
        # Zero out the 16 bytes of MA data (after type=1 byte + length=1 byte)
        for i in range(16):
            packed_for_calc[ma_start + 2 + i] = 0
            
        print("Packet for calculation (MA zeroed):", bytes(packed_for_calc).hex())
        
        # Calculate HMAC-MD5
        secret = "SECRET"
        calculated_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), bytes(packed_for_calc), 'md5').digest()
        print("Calculated Message-Authenticator:", calculated_ma.hex())
        print("Original Message-Authenticator: ", original_ma.hex())
        print("Match:", calculated_ma == original_ma)
        
        # What if we calculate on the original packet?
        wrong_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), packed_original, 'md5').digest()
        print("Wrong calculation (on original):", wrong_ma.hex())

    @staticmethod
    def parse(packed_message, secret, radius_lifecycle=None):
        """
        Args:
            packed_message:
            secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
            radius_lifecycle: RadiusLifecycle object
        Returns:
            RadiusPacket - RadiusAccessChallenge/RadiusAccessRequest/
                            RadiusAccessAccept/RadiusAccessFailure
        Raises:
            MessageParseError: if packed_message cannot be parsed
        """
        try:
            code, packet_id, length, response_authenticator = struct.unpack("!BBH16s",
                                                                            packed_message[:RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH])
        except struct.error as exception:
            raise MessageParseError('Unable to unpack first 20 bytes of RADIUS header') \
                from exception

        response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(response_authenticator)
        if code in PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS.keys():
            radius_packet = PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS[code](packet_id, response_authenticator,
                                                      RadiusAttributesList.parse(
                                                          packed_message[RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH:]))
            try:
                request_authenticator = radius_lifecycle.packet_id_to_request_authenticator[packet_id]
            except KeyError as exception:
                raise MessageParseError('Unknown RAIDUS packet_id: %s' % packet_id,) \
                    from exception
            try:
                return radius_packet.validate_packet(secret,
>                                                    request_authenticator=request_authenticator)

    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):
        """Calculates the Response Authenticator (in Radius Header) and
        MessageAuthenticator (a Radius Attribute) hashes and compares with what was provided.
        Args:
            secret (str): secret shared between RADIUS and chewie.
            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
             packet (which is a response)
        Raises:
            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
        """
        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
        # get the Original Message Authenticator

        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        response_validation_needed = request_authenticator is not None
        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        message_validation_needed = message_authenticator is not None

        # If no validation is needed, return early
        if not response_validation_needed and not message_validation_needed:
            return self

        if not secret:
            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        if response_validation_needed:
            response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
            radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
            radius_packet.pack()
            calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
                                                                 bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
            if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:

                    "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
                        response_authenticator,
                        calculated_response_authenticator,
                        binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))

    @staticmethod
    def parse(packed_message, secret, radius_lifecycle=None):
        """
        Args:
            packed_message:
            secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
            radius_lifecycle: RadiusLifecycle object
        Returns:
            RadiusPacket - RadiusAccessChallenge/RadiusAccessRequest/
                            RadiusAccessAccept/RadiusAccessFailure
        Raises:
            MessageParseError: if packed_message cannot be parsed
        """
        try:
            code, packet_id, length, response_authenticator = struct.unpack("!BBH16s",
                                                                            packed_message[:RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH])
        except struct.error as exception:
            raise MessageParseError('Unable to unpack first 20 bytes of RADIUS header') \
                from exception

        response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(response_authenticator)
        if code in PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS.keys():
            radius_packet = PACKET_TYPE_PARSERS[code](packet_id, response_authenticator,
                                                      RadiusAttributesList.parse(
                                                          packed_message[RADIUS_HEADER_LENGTH:]))
            try:
                request_authenticator = radius_lifecycle.packet_id_to_request_authenticator[packet_id]
            except KeyError as exception:
                raise MessageParseError('Unknown RAIDUS packet_id: %s' % packet_id,) \
                    from exception
            try:
                return radius_packet.validate_packet(secret,
                                                     request_authenticator=request_authenticator)
            except (InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError,
                    InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError) as exception:
                raise MessageParseError("Unable to parse Radius packet") \

    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):
        """Calculates the Response Authenticator (in Radius Header) and
        MessageAuthenticator (a Radius Attribute) hashes and compares with what was provided.
        Args:
            secret (str): secret shared between RADIUS and chewie.
            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
             packet (which is a response)
        Raises:
            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
        """
        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
        # get the Original Message Authenticator

        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        response_validation_needed = request_authenticator is not None
        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        message_validation_needed = message_authenticator is not None

        # If no validation is needed, return early
        if not response_validation_needed and not message_validation_needed:
            return self

        if not secret:
            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        if response_validation_needed:
            response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
            radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
            radius_packet.pack()
            calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
                                                                 bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
            if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:
                raise InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
                        response_authenticator,
                        calculated_response_authenticator,
                        binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))

        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        if message_validation_needed:
            original_ma = message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data
            # Replace the Original Message Authenticator
            message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data = bytes.fromhex("00000000000000000000000000000000")
            radius_packet.pack()

            # calculate new hash message authenticator
            new_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), radius_packet.packed, 'md5').digest()

            # compare old and new message authenticator
            if original_ma != new_ma:
                # DEBUG: Let's see what the packet actually looks like
                ma_start = radius_packet.packed.find(b'\x50\x12')  # Find Message-Authenticator
                if ma_start != -1:
                    current_ma_in_packet = radius_packet.packed[ma_start+2:ma_start+18]
                    print(f"DEBUG: MA field in packed packet: {current_ma_in_packet.hex()}")
                    # Create properly zeroed packet
                    temp_packet = bytearray(radius_packet.packed)
                    for i in range(16):
                        temp_packet[ma_start + 2 + i] = 0
                    recalculated_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), bytes(temp_packet), 'md5').digest()
                    print(f"DEBUG: Recalculated with manual zeroing: {recalculated_ma.hex()}")

                raise InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original Message-Authenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s'" %
>                   (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))

            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
             packet (which is a response)
        Raises:
            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
        """
        # TEMPORARY: Use original logic for debugging
        if request_authenticator is None:
            # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
            radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
            # get the Original Message Authenticator

            message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)
            if not message_authenticator or not request_authenticator:
                # no validation required.
                return self
            # This should never be reached with request_authenticator=None
            # But let's see what happens...
        
        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
        # get the Original Message Authenticator

        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)
        
        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        response_validation_needed = request_authenticator is not None
        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided  
        message_validation_needed = message_authenticator is not None
        
        # If no validation is needed, return early
        if not response_validation_needed and not message_validation_needed:
            return self
            
        if not secret:
            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        if response_validation_needed:
            response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
            radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
            radius_packet.pack()
            calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
                                                                 bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
            if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:
                raise InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
                        response_authenticator,
                        calculated_response_authenticator,
                        binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))

        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        if message_validation_needed:
            original_ma = message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data
            # Replace the Original Message Authenticator
            message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data = bytes.fromhex("00000000000000000000000000000000")
            radius_packet.pack()

            # calculate new hash message authenticator
            new_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), radius_packet.packed, 'md5').digest()

            # compare old and new message authenticator
            if original_ma != new_ma:
                # DEBUG: Let's see what the packet actually looks like
                ma_start = radius_packet.packed.find(b'\x50\x12')  # Find Message-Authenticator
                if ma_start != -1:
                    current_ma_in_packet = radius_packet.packed[ma_start+2:ma_start+18]
                    print(f"DEBUG: MA field in packed packet: {current_ma_in_packet.hex()}")
                    # Create properly zeroed packet
                    temp_packet = bytearray(radius_packet.packed)
                    for i in range(16):
                        temp_packet[ma_start + 2 + i] = 0
                    recalculated_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), bytes(temp_packet), 'md5').digest()
                    print(f"DEBUG: Recalculated with manual zeroing: {recalculated_ma.hex()}")
                
                raise InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original Message-Authenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s'" %
                    (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))
        return self</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):

But this goes against the issue description which says we should still check the request_authenticator (and by implication, we should still check the message_authenticator when it exists).

Let me re-read the issue more carefully...

Actually, wait. The issue says:
> If Message-Authenticator doesn't exist we should still check the request_authenticator

This means:
- Case 1: Message-Authenticator EXISTS but request_authenticator is MISSING → should validate Message-Authenticator  
- Case 2: Message-Authenticator is MISSING but request_authenticator EXISTS → should validate request_authenticator
- Case 3: Both exist → should validate both
- Case 4: Both missing → no validation needed

But the failing test is:
- Message-Authenticator EXISTS but request_authenticator is None → should validate Message-Authenticator

So my logic is correct in principle. The issue must be in my implementation of the Message-Authenticator validation.

Let me remove the temporary hack and fix the real issue. The problem is probably that I'm calculating the HMAC on the wrong packet. Let me compare with the working debug example.

From my debug script:
- Original packet with MA=`73f...573` 
- Zeroed packet produces HMAC=`73f...573` (correct!)

But in my implementation:
- Same original packet
- Zeroed packet produces HMAC=`dd298af8e1b661eb96eb936f776c8b43` (wrong!)

This suggests that the packet I'm working with is somehow different. Maybe the deepcopy isn't preserving the original packet correctly, or there's some other difference.

Let me look at this more systematically. The issue might be that the `self` object (the original RadiusPacket) has already been processed somehow.     def parse(packed_message, secret, radius_lifecycle=None):
        """
        Args:
            packed_message:
            secret (str): Shared sceret between chewie and RADIUS server.
            radius_lifecycle: RadiusLifecycle object
        Returns:
            RadiusPacket - RadiusAccessChallenge/RadiusAccessRequest/

self = <chewie.radius.RadiusAccessRequest object at 0x7c234d1ab0b8>, secret = 'SECRET', request_authenticator = None

    def validate_packet(self, secret, request_authenticator=None):
        """Calculates the Response Authenticator (in Radius Header) and
        MessageAuthenticator (a Radius Attribute) hashes and compares with what was provided.
        Args:
            secret (str): secret shared between RADIUS and chewie.
            request_authenticator (): the original request authenticator for this
             packet (which is a response)
        Raises:
            ValueError: if secret is None or empty string.
            InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError: if Response Authenticator does not match calculated.
            InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: if MessageAuthenticator does not match calculated.
        """
        # Copy this packet so we can modify the 'Authenticator' and 'Message-Authenticator'
        radius_packet = copy.deepcopy(self)
        # get the Original Message Authenticator

        message_authenticator = radius_packet.attributes.find(MessageAuthenticator.DESCRIPTION)

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        response_validation_needed = request_authenticator is not None
        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        message_validation_needed = message_authenticator is not None

        # If no validation is needed, return early
        if not response_validation_needed and not message_validation_needed:
            return self

        if not secret:
            raise ValueError("secret cannot be None for hashing")

        # Validate response authenticator if request_authenticator is provided
        if response_validation_needed:
            response_authenticator = radius_packet.authenticator
            radius_packet.authenticator = request_authenticator
            radius_packet.pack()
            calculated_response_authenticator = binascii.hexlify(hashlib.md5(radius_packet.packed +
                                                                 bytearray(secret, 'utf-8')).digest())
            if calculated_response_authenticator != response_authenticator:
                raise InvalidResponseAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original ResponseAuthenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s' %s" % (
                        response_authenticator,
                        calculated_response_authenticator,
                        binascii.hexlify(radius_packet.packed)))

        # Validate message authenticator if message_authenticator is provided
        if message_validation_needed:
            original_ma = message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data
            # DEBUG: Check what we're working with
            print(f"DEBUG: Working with radius_packet.packed length: {len(radius_packet.packed) if hasattr(radius_packet, 'packed') and radius_packet.packed else 'None'}")
            if hasattr(radius_packet, 'packed') and radius_packet.packed:
                ma_start = radius_packet.packed.find(b'\x50\x12')
                if ma_start != -1:
                    ma_in_packet = radius_packet.packed[ma_start+2:ma_start+18]
                    print(f"DEBUG: MA in radius_packet before zeroing: {ma_in_packet.hex()}")

            # Replace the Original Message Authenticator
            message_authenticator.data_type.bytes_data = bytes.fromhex("00000000000000000000000000000000")
            radius_packet.pack()

            # calculate new hash message authenticator
            new_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), radius_packet.packed, 'md5').digest()

            # compare old and new message authenticator
            if original_ma != new_ma:
                # DEBUG: Let's see what the packet actually looks like
                ma_start = radius_packet.packed.find(b'\x50\x12')  # Find Message-Authenticator
                if ma_start != -1:
                    current_ma_in_packet = radius_packet.packed[ma_start+2:ma_start+18]
                    print(f"DEBUG: MA field in packed packet: {current_ma_in_packet.hex()}")
                    # Create properly zeroed packet
                    temp_packet = bytearray(radius_packet.packed)
                    for i in range(16):
                        temp_packet[ma_start + 2 + i] = 0
                    recalculated_ma = hmac.new(secret.encode(), bytes(temp_packet), 'md5').digest()
                    print(f"DEBUG: Recalculated with manual zeroing: {recalculated_ma.hex()}")
                    print(f"DEBUG: Expected original: {original_ma.hex()}")

                raise InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError(
                    "Original Message-Authenticator: '%s', does not match calculated: '%s'" %
>                   (binascii.hexlify(original_ma), binascii.hexlify(new_ma)))
E               chewie.radius.InvalidMessageAuthenticatorError: Original Message-Authenticator: 'b'73f...573'', does not match calculated: 'b'dd298af8e1b661eb96eb936f776c8b43''

```
"""Only call this once, or else the MessageAuthenticator will not be zeros,

```

This suggests that the Message-Authenticator should be calculated on a packet where the MA field is zeroed out from the beginning.
